With my changing shifts, I got to where I’d only sleep a few hours and still feel kind of wired. And now, even when I’m exhausted, I wake up really early and my mind is already going.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s been kind of all over the place. I mean, I’ve had rough times before, but I don’t know if it was exactly like this. This feels… heavier than what I remember.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About a month, I think. I’ve had thoughts like maybe everybody would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. And a few times walking home, I caught myself thinking about stepping off the curb into traffic. It scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of not wanting to live or hurting yourself included any actual plans or preparations to act on those thoughts?\n\nPatient: I haven’t made any real plan or done anything to prepare. It’s more like… I get these scary thoughts, like maybe everyone would be better off if I didn’t wake up. And a few times walking home, I caught myself thinking about stepping off the curb into traffic, but I haven’t set out to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have these thoughts of not wanting to live or hurting yourself been happening every day?\n\nPatient: Not every single day, no… but pretty often. The “not waking up” thought comes up a lot, and a few times when I’m walking home I’ve had that thought about stepping into traffic. It scares me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have these thoughts of not wanting to live or hurting yourself been interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has. When those thoughts come up, like not wanting to wake up or thinking about stepping into traffic on the way home, it really shakes me up. I get scared and distracted, and it can make it hard to focus on getting through the day.\n\nDoctor: Have these thoughts of not wanting to live or hurting yourself been getting worse over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they have been a little worse this past month.
